5|## Statement
6|
7|For every group G, finite-index subgroups U_1..U_7 (possibly equal) and pairwise disjoint LEFT cosets g_iU_i, some pair i≠j has gcd([G:U_i],[G:U_j])≥7. No cover, finite-original-group, normality, solvability, bounded-valuation or bounded-cofactor hypothesis. This is Zhi-Wei Sun's 2004 conjecture at k=7 (Zhu proved k=3,4 in 2008; see the priority records for limits of that comparison).

1|# HIGH-K1: a uniform terminal-kappa-one exclusion and the entire remaining high-terminal core
2|
3|CANDIDATE SCOPED PARTIAL, written by hand. Full terminal k>=p+2 exclusion is NOT proved. No full ordered
4|Olson/every-avoider composition, independent discovery/corroboration, strict preregistration, priority,
5|human/Lean/kernel or runtime claim. Parent alone runs new reviews/verdicts.
6|
7|## 0. Corrected scientific scope and Step-0 disposition
8|
9|The original operative scope is governed by directory/deadline addendum1 and scientific addendum2; both
10|numbered worker acknowledgements and permanent process classifications are integral packet inputs. The
11|parent's 'all terminal k<=p NFs excluded/only remaining gap/o_F equivalence' preview is WITHDRAWN.
12|Literal ASSEMBLY-LS LF11 and ASSEMBLY-P LF13 give ROTATION STRUCTURE, not NF exclusions; their word
13|conclusions retain original o_F<=p-1 or<=p. Original o_F>p with a terminal k<=p is not removed by those
14|interfaces. The audited v8 union closes k=p+1 at its exact existential NF antecedent, not every avoider.
15|The UPAR5 boundary erratum2 withdraws the malformed high-k-vacuity aside; it changes no UPAR5 mathematics.
16|
17|Here p>=7 prime, w has length4p-2 over D_(2p) and no identity2p-subword in increasing ORIGINAL index order.
18|Investigate SOME admissible NF u with terminal k>=p+2. Keep arbitrary extra odd-block pairs, genuine empty
19|runs, all actual NF domains, and every original o_F compatible with NF. No o_F/nonempty-run/rotation-pair/
20|rigidity assumption. This is ONE independent remaining NF class, NOT all remaining word-level work.
21|No k=p+1 UMR/UPAR integral lifts, central windows or bipartite frames are used at a different k.
22|
23|NEW CLAIM HK1: relative to accepted S(p) at SECONDARY-STATEMENT trust, no such NF has kappa_hat=1, uniformly
24|for every prime p>=7 and EVERY k>=p+2. The proof also gives the separate p=5 kappa_hat1 statement at the same
25|import trust; no computer-assisted p5 lift is used. Baseline kappa_hat0 is already VAC/P, no duplicate credit.
26|CLAIM CORE: the ENTIRE remaining p>=7 target induces the exact necessary residue system of Sec.6, with
27|2<=kappa_hat<=p-1, not a selected-cell or selected-frame substitute. Its full cover remains UNPROVED.
28|
29|The earlier UPAR4 pre-freeze PROCESS MAJOR and both current pre-ack PROCESS MINORs remain PERMANENT/
30|UNCURED. Later source checks, proofs or reviews do not cure them. The parent path/deadline clerical defect
31|and lower-terminal documentary overclaim remain recorded separately. No acknowledgement/reset loop is used.
32|
33|## 1. Literal general-k NF, GRX and accepted baseline
34|
35|Write kappa:=kappa_hat for the NORMAL-FORM parameter, not original kappa(w). NF preserves avoidance,
36|length,F and the rotation multiset from w to u only; no inverse avoidance-preservation is assumed. Its cyclic
37|representation is R_1 S_1 ... R_k S_k, S_i=sigma_(y_i)^(2h_i+1), h_i>=0; r_i=|R_i|>=0, and every r_i=0
38|has y_(i-1)!=y_i. Set
39|  H=sum h_i=(F-k)/2=p-1-kappa, F=k+2H, R=sum r_i=2p+2kappa-k.
40|These are verified GENERAL-k NF identities, not p+1 specialisations. In particular kappa<=p-1. GRX all-plus
41|gives kappa>=0 and ZP_kappa on the odd rotation lift Y(x)=(1,x) in C_2 x C_p=C_(2p). Every zero-sum of odd
42|terms has even size. NF also gives1<=k<=o_F(w),k=o_F(w) mod2, and
43|kappa_hat=kappa(w)-(o_F(w)-k)/2. None is a reverse o_F bound; DAV kappa_hat<=k-1 does not upper-bound k.
44|
45|For z in{+1,-1}^k put f_i=[z_i!=z_(i+1)], Phi=sum f_i (even), and
46|  T(z)=sum_(z_i=+1)(y_(i-1)-y_i) mod p, X^z=union_i z_i X_i,
47|where X_i is the actual rotation-value occurrence multiset in run i. Since every block is odd, GRX has
48|C_max=F-(k-Phi)=2H+Phi. Therefore an identity2p-selection exists EXACTLY when some z and EVEN n satisfy
49|  max(0,2kappa+2-Phi)<=n<=min(R,2p-Phi),   T(z) in Sigma_n(X^z).                    (G)
50|An empty size interval gives no test. Sigma_0={0}. This is freshly derived from GENERAL GRX by n=2p-C,
51|not a p+1 lift. For a nonempty proper cyclic interval P of runs, z=-1 on P has Phi2 and
52|T(z)=y_(j')-y_j at its boundary blocks; in particular equal boundary values give T=0.
53|
54|Theorem P applies to u at its literal length<=4p-1,F>=1 scope. Its n_t are rotation-GAP LENGTHS between
55|consecutive reflection occurrences, NOT multiplicities of rotation values. In u these are r_i plus internal
56|zero gaps, so their floor sum is Pgap:=sum_i floor(r_i/2). Any actual avoider necessarily has
57|  F+Pgap<=3p-2, hence Pgap<=p+2kappa-k and k<=p+2kappa.                            (P-fail)
58|The region k>=p+1+2kappa is thus old P coverage, not a new result. VAC at its literal p>=7,k>=p+1 scope also
59|excludes kappa0. At kappa1 all k>=p+3 are P-covered. Only k=p+2 needs the new argument.
60|
61|At this critical family k=p+2,kappa1 we have
62|  H=p-2,F=3p-2,R=p,Pgap=0.
63|Consequently every r_i is0or1, exactly p gaps are nonempty and two are empty. There are p maximal CYCLIC
64|REFLECTION RUNS separated by those p single rotation letters. A rho_0 is a rotation LETTER, not an empty
65|gap; do not delete or merge across it without proof. All-plus(G) excludes every even rotation zero-sum of
66|length>=4. Since |Y|=p is odd, either Y is zero-sum-free in C_(2p), or it has a zero-sum PAIR.
67|
68|## 2. Odd-block pair padding and a cyclic color lemma
69|
70|In any all-odd NF, selecting zero or one representative reflection from each block leaves h_i disjoint
71|same-value pairs available in that block, independently of its selected parity. Any integer0..H is a sum
72|of choices0..h_i. Thus an identity skeleton selection of EVEN length L with
73|  2kappa+2=2p-2H<=L<=2p
74|can be padded with(2p-L)/2 pairs to identity2p in u. Conversely deleting selected even pairs from a2p
75|identity selection gives such a skeleton selection. This is selection bookkeeping, not a new word mutation.
76|The skeleton has length R+k=2p+2kappa and a DIFFERENT forbidden-length interval; it is not a p+1 normal form.
77|Cyclic cuts conjugate products and preserve identity in the original index order.
78|
79|In the critical kappa1 family, any pure representative-reflection identity of length4or6 is therefore
80|forbidden (6<=2p for p>=5). We need this elementary COLOR fact:
81|
82|A cyclic sequence of p+2 reflection values over F_p, each value occurring at most twice, cannot avoid all
83|pure reflection identities4and6. Indeed two doubled values A,B must INTERLACE: AABB and ABBA already have
84|product identity. If three values double, pairwise interlacing gives ABCABC up to relabelling/cyclic cut.
85|Its product is(sigma_A sigma_B sigma_C)^2=1, since an odd product of reflections is a reflection. Hence at
86|most two values double. p+2 positions over p values then force exactly two doubles A,B and all p values
87|present, in cyclic order ABAB on those four positions. The value C=2A-B is distinct from A,B since p is odd,
88|so it is present once. The two A-to-A arcs each contain a B. Choose the arc NOT containing C: the resulting
89|cyclic selection A,B,A,C has product rho_(2A-B-C)=1, forbidden. This proves the COLOR fact.               []
90|
91|## 3. Critical kappa1 when Y has a zero-sum pair
92|
93|Let the pair be rotation values x,-x. If x=0, the k>p block values contain two equal values; their interval
94|pattern has Phi2,T0, and the two zero rotations are a signed sum0 for either sign. At kappa1,n2 satisfies(G),
95|giving identity2p, contradiction.
96|
97|If x!=0 and a reflection value occurred at least three times, take three consecutive occurrences of that
98|value around the cycle. Their three intervening run arcs partition the rotation occurrences; one contains
99|neither marked rotation. Its endpoints have equal value, so its Phi2 pattern has T0 and the two marked
100|rotations lie in the SAME sign zone. Their signed sum is0. Again n2 is admissible in(G), contradiction.
101|Thus every reflection value occurs at most twice. The COLOR fact now supplies a pure4or6 identity, which
102|pair padding turns into identity2p in u. This closes the entire zero-sum-pair case, with no S(p) premise. []
103|
104|## 4. Critical kappa1 when Y is zero-sum-free
105|
106|Use the ACCEPTED classical S(p) statement: a zero-sum-free sequence of p odd terms of C_(2p) has an odd
107|generator g with least positive ODD coefficients a_i summing W<=2p-1. This is imported at secondary-restatement
108|trust (Xia-Yuan through Peng-Sun, primary proof unread), NOT claimed proved or upgraded here. Write
109|u=#coefficient1 terms, s=#larger-coefficient terms, so u+s=p. The word 'one' here refers to COEFFICIENT1,
110|not all group generators. Put E=W-p<=p-1. If s>=2, every larger coefficient b satisfies
111|  b<=E-2(s-1)+1<=p-2s+2<=p-s=u.
112|If s=1, b is odd and b<=p. The only case with b>u=p-1 is b=p, namely
113|  Y=g^(p-1)(pg).                                                                (X)
114|If s=0 all coefficients are1.
115|
116|Outside(X), EVERY bipartition of Y into two nonempty parts admits an EVEN signed zero-sum of size2..p-1.
117|If coefficient-one terms occur in both parts, choose one on each side. Otherwise all u such terms are on
118|one side. Choose a term b from the other side and b coefficient-one terms from the first; their signed
119|coefficients cancel exactly, length b+1 even. If s>=2, b+1<=u+1<=p-1; if s=1 outside(X), odd b<=p-2 gives
120|the same bound. The s=0 case necessarily split ones. All choices use actual distinct occurrences.
121|
122|Suppose a reflection value appeared in two DIFFERENT maximal reflection runs of u. Both cyclic arcs between
123|those blocks contain rotations, so they give a bipartition of Y into two nonempty parts. The equal-boundary
124|Phi2 pattern has T0. The preceding cancellation gives a signed even rotation selection of size2..p-1,
125|which lies inside(G)'s Phi2 interval[2,min(p,2p-2)]. Contradiction. Therefore the color palettes of the p
126|maximal reflection runs are disjoint. Each palette is nonempty; k=p+2>p and the genuine empty rule force
127|at least one run to have at least two different block values. The palettes would require at least p+1 colors
128|in F_p, impossible. This closes the nonexceptional zero-sum-free case without imposing rotation rigidity. []
129|
130|For(X), g=(1,c) has c!=0, so rotations are rho_c^(p-1),rho_0. Choose a sign pattern with FOUR changes;
131|it exists on the cycle k=p+2>=7 (take two nonadjacent runs as the minus set). Let A,B count the c-letters
132|with positive/negative signs, A+B=p-1. Every integer d in[-B,A] is obtained using |d| signed c-letters;
133|include the one zero rotation exactly when |d| is odd. This gives an EVEN size0..p-1 and signed sum dc.
134|There are p consecutive integers d, so every residue of F_p is obtained. At Phi4,kappa1, all these even
135|sizes lie in(G): lower0, upper min(p,2p-4)>=p-1. Some selection has signed sum T(z), contradiction.
136|This is a FRESH general-GRX four-change argument; old ARC0's different zero multiplicities are not imported. []
137|
138|Sections3-4 exhaust Y. This proves HK1 for all primes p>=5 at the declared S(p) trust. In particular the
139|agreed p>=7 diagnostic family k=p+2,kappa_hat1 is excluded; all larger k at kappa1 were P-covered. No special
140|k=p+1 lift/window/frame, no arbitrary gap suppression, no rotation-pair or original o_F hypothesis was added.
141|The separate p=5 statement concerns ONLY this kappa1 NF family; no full p5 or finite-computation claim.
142|
143|## 5. The top diagnostic: proved necessary structure, NOT exclusion
144|
145|Now p>=7,k=p+2,kappa=p-1. The verified NF formulas give H0, every block SINGLETON, F=k,R=3p-4. The full
146|residual is the specialization of Sec.6, not merely the tests below. Here(G) has the EXACT rotation size
147|  n=2p-Phi
148|for every sign pattern (all such sizes are available, since0<=Phi<=p+1 and R>=2p). The all-plus odd Y has
149|ZP_(p-1). Accepted CT at n_CT=p-4>=1, |Y|-n_CT=2p, therefore gives
150|  sum_a min(p-4,v_a(Y))<=2p-6.                                                   (CT-top)
151|This is CT on the ORIGINAL all-plus Y only, not on arbitrary signed multisets without their own ZP.
152|
153|For EVERY pattern Phi>=4 a signed value has multiplicity at least2p-2. Here is the complete new-range
154|calculation, not an application of the old k=p+1 Lemma P. A multiset of size N,maximal multiplicity mu has
155|q disjoint unequal-value pairs if2q<=N and q<=N-mu: cap multiplicities at q, whose total is>=2q (the largest
156|keeps q if necessary and the other caps retain at least q); take2q items with each value consecutive and
157|pair positions i,i+q. Equality would fill q+1 equal positions, impossible. This proves the generic pair lemma.
158|
159|If signed mu<=2p-3, apply it to R=3p-4 with q=p-1. Phi>=4 gives n=2p-Phi in[p-1,2p-4]. Outside the q
160|pairs lie p-2 items; choose n-(p-1) in[0,p-3] fillers. Taking one item from each unequal pair gives sums
161|base+sum_(i in I)d_i, with p-1 nonzero differences d_i. Iterated classical Cauchy-Davenport fills all F_p.
162|Some actual n-rotation selection sums to T(z), violating(G). Thus mu>=2p-2 as asserted.
163|
164|The heavy value is unique since2(2p-2)>R. Its ORIGINAL unsigned value class contains at least2p-2 letters.
165|These classes coincide over all Phi>=4 patterns: two different unsigned classes are disjoint and would
166|exceed R. Denote the common class{a,-a}. It is nonzero: otherwise at least2p-2 original rotations are zero;
167|k>p gives two equal block values, whose Phi2,T0 pattern admits precisely n=2p-2 zero rotations, contradiction.
168|Hence at most p-2 rotations lie outside one NONZERO unsigned pair, and each Phi>=4 pattern has a signed
169|value in that pair of multiplicity>=2p-2. This is NEAR-PAIR structure, NOT a claim all rotations lie in a pair.
170|At Phi2, n=2p-2 would require p-1 fillers after the same q pairs, but only p-2 are available: the argument
171|fails by ONE filler. It is not a no-go theorem. No full top-family exclusion or feasible tuple is claimed.
172|
173|## 6. ENTIRE remaining high-terminal core, not a diagnostic substitute
174|
175|DEFINE HIGH-K-RES for p>=7 by the following FULL intersection on actual admissible NF data. It retains the
176|whole original independent target, after old baseline and HK1 exclusions:
177|(R0) 2<=kappa<=p-1, p+2<=k<=p+2kappa; H=p-1-kappa,F=k+2H,R=2p+2kappa-k. Give k cyclic blocks with
178|h_i>=0,sum h_i=H,ell_i=2h_i+1 and r_i>=0,sum r_i=R; all rotation occurrence values X_i in F_p (zero allowed),
179|block values y_i in F_p; every r_i=0 has y_(i-1)!=y_i. Original-to-NF avoidance/length/F/rotation-multiset
180|preservation and the existential admissible-NF antecedent remain. k<=o_F(w),k=o_F(w) mod2 are ONLY one-way
181|relations; no original o_F upper bound or every-avoider composition is added.
182|(R1) Retain the FULL gap-count failure Pgap=sum floor(r_i/2)<=p+2kappa-k, not a multiplicity substitute or only
183|its scalar k upper bound. Retain the actual general-NF/DAV consequences at their accepted domains; here
184|kappa<=k-1 is already redundant. R>=p follows from the upper k bound, not an extra assumption.
185|(R2) For EVERY sign pattern z, compute Phi,T(z),X^z ACTUALLY from these data. For EVERY EVEN n in the exact
186|interval of(G), require T(z) not in Sigma_n(X^z). No arc-only, all-plus-only, small-m or zero-T replacement.
187|This includes all-plus ZP_kappa. No special p+1 R_PAR/R2/UPAR integral constraint is imposed at the new k.
188|(R3) Any used sequence CT test retains its exact domain: on the all-plus odd Y, for EACH integer1<=q<=R with
189|R-q even in[2kappa+2,2p], require sum_a min(q,v_a(Y))<=p+q-2. No q0 or signed-ZP hypothesis is invented.
190|The top-family tests of Sec.5 are additional proved necessary consequences, not a substitute for(R0)-(R3).
191|
192|Every hypothetical remaining actual NF has all this data by literal NF/GRX/P, accepted CT and HK1. This is
193|finite for EACH fixed p (bounded k,H,R,finite residue fields), not an asserted finite unbounded union. No
194|nonempty residual, counterexample, scalar-table feasibility or inverse NF assembly is claimed. Complete
195|(G) is the accepted exact word-level criterion; other necessary consequences may further shrink the data.
196|
197|For the agreed diagnostic k=p+2,kappa=p-1, specialize(R0)-(R3): H0,ell_i1,R=3p-4; sum floor(r_i/2)<=2p-4;
198|for every z require T(z) not in Sigma_(2p-Phi)(X^z); retain(CT-top) and Sec.5's Phi>=4 common nonzero near-pair
199|structure. This is the ENTIRE remaining specialization, not a feasible configuration or an excluded family.
200|
201|FIRST UNPROVED LEMMA, HIGH-K-CORE-COVER: no actual target NF anywhere on the ENTIRE unbounded p>=7
202|HIGH-K-RES exists. This is NOT proved. It includes all k in the displayed remaining band/all kappa>=2, not
203|only k=p+2 or the top diagnostic. Full terminal k>=p+2 exclusion and all full-Olson compositions remain open.
204|
205|| Agreed uniform family, all p>=7 | Precise outcome |

214|alone. This is an INTERNAL coverage statement, not novelty/priority or independence from earlier methods.

### SOURCE 17: proof-theoremP.md
Path:/Users/jinleic/repos/aifours/mathematics/evidence/six-lane-20261016/olson/proof-theoremP.md
Whole-file SHA256:58a85ebeba8208e2f89b39a691235d2d1374656d8888ea905fe96aa75e1dafc2
Bytes:5964; complete original line range:1-109
1|# Theorem P: a Chevalley–Warning proof of ordered EGZ for a class of dihedral words
2|
3|Status: written proof (lane MathOlson, 2026-10-16), mechanism checked by brute
4|force (`code/verify_marked.py`, `code/verify_theoremP.py`); NOT yet independently
5|reviewed.  This is a theorem about a CLASS OF WORDS in D_{2p}; it does not
6|prove ordered Olson for D_{2p} (the "many odd gaps" class remains open here).
7|
8|## Setting and conventions
9|
10|p is an odd prime. D_{2p} = {(v, e) : v in Z_p, e in {+1,-1}} with
11|(v, e)(v', e') = (v + e v', e e')  (rotations (x,+1), reflections (y,-1)).
12|For a word w = w_1 ... w_N and indices i_1 < ... < i_k the ordered product is
13|w_{i_1} w_{i_2} ... w_{i_k}.  An identity k-subword is such an index set with
14|product (0, +1).  Cyclic rotation of w preserves the existence of identity
15|k-subwords (a product U V becomes V U = U^{-1} (U V) U).
16|
17|Let w contain F >= 1 reflections.  Rotate w cyclically so that it ENDS with a
18|reflection:
19|    w = X_1 f_1 X_2 f_2 ... X_F f_F,
20|where f_t = (y_t, -1) are the reflections and X_t (t = 1..F) is the block of
21|rotations strictly between f_{t-1} and f_t (X_1 = rotations before f_1); n_t =
22||X_t|.  ("Gap t" = X_t; cyclically these are exactly the gaps between
23|consecutive reflections.)
24|
25|## Theorem P
26|
27|If N = |w| <= 4p - 1 and  F + sum_{t=1}^{F} floor(n_t / 2) >= 3p - 1,  then w
28|has an identity 2p-subword.
29|
30|## Proof
31|
32|**Step 1 (parametrisation).** In each gap X_t list the rotations in index order
33|and group them into floor(n_t/2) consecutive PAIRS pi = (i, i'); if n_t is odd
34|the last rotation of X_t is a SINGLETON.  Introduce Boolean parameters
35|  u_2, ..., u_F  (gap colours; set u_1 := 0 and u_{F+1} := u_1 = 0),
36|  m_pi  for each pair pi  (pair marks).
37|Number of parameters:  M = (F - 1) + sum_t floor(n_t/2) >= 3p - 2.
38|
39|To a parameter point (u, m) assign the selection S(u, m):
40|* reflection f_t is selected iff u_t != u_{t+1}  (t = 1..F);
41|* both rotations of pair pi in gap t are selected iff m_pi XOR u_t = 1,
42|  otherwise neither;
43|* singletons are never selected.
44|
45|**Step 2 (injectivity; the empty selection).** The selection determines the
46|parameters: going left to right, u_1 = 0 and u_{t+1} = u_t XOR [f_t selected]
47|determine all u_t; then m_pi = [pi selected] XOR u_t.  Hence the map
48|(u, m) -> S(u, m) is injective, and S(u, m) is EMPTY iff (u, m) = 0: an empty
49|selection selects no reflection, so all u_t = u_1 = 0, and no pair, so all
50|m_pi = u_t = 0.
51|
52|**Step 3 (size).** |S(u,m)| = #{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} + 2 #{pi : m_pi XOR u_{t(pi)} = 1}.
53|The first term is the number of colour changes around the cycle 1..F, 1, hence
54|even.  So |S| is always EVEN, and |S| <= N <= 4p - 1.
55|
56|**Step 4 (signs and the product).** Let S = S(u,m).  For an element of S lying
57|in gap t or equal to f_t, the number of selected reflections strictly before
58|it is #{s < t : u_s != u_{s+1}}, which has the parity of [u_t != u_1] = u_t.
59|So in the ordered product every selected rotation x in gap t is multiplied by
60|c_t := (-1)^{u_t}, and a selected reflection f_t contributes c_t y_t to the
61|rotation coordinate.  The number of selected reflections is even (Step 3), so
62|the product is (V, +1) with
63|  V = sum_{t : u_t != u_{t+1}} c_t y_t + sum_{pairs pi selected} c_{t(pi)} x_pi,
64|where x_pi = x_i + x_i'.  Using (c_t - c_{t+1})/2 = u_{t+1} - u_t (which is
65|0 when u_t = u_{t+1} and c_t when they differ) and summation by parts with
66|u_1 = u_{F+1} = 0,
67|  sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_{t+1} - u_t) y_t = sum_{t=2}^{F} u_t (y_{t-1} - y_t).
68|For pairs: a selected pair (m XOR u_t = 1) contributes c_t x_pi; one checks
69|c_t x_pi [m XOR u_t] = x_pi m - x_pi u_t for both u_t = 0, 1.  Therefore, in Z_p,
70|  V(u, m) = sum_{t=2}^{F} beta_t u_t + sum_pi x_pi m_pi,
71|  beta_t = y_{t-1} - y_t - sum_{pi in gap t} x_pi.
72|V is a polynomial of degree 1 in the parameters (over F_p), and S(u,m) is an
73|identity subword iff V(u, m) = 0 in Z_p.
74|
75|**Step 5 (Chevalley–Warning on the Boolean box).** Write the size as the
76|integer polynomial
77|  Z(u, m) = sum_{t=1}^{F} (u_t + u_{t+1} - 2 u_t u_{t+1}) + 2 sum_pi (m_pi + u_{t(pi)} - 2 m_pi u_{t(pi)}),
78|which agrees with |S(u, m)| on {0,1}^M and has degree 2.  Over F_p put
79|  Q = (1 - V^{p-1}) (1 - Z^{p-1}),   deg Q <= 3(p - 1) < M.
80|On {0,1}^M, Q(x) = 1 if V(x) = 0 and Z(x) = 0 in F_p, and Q(x) = 0 otherwise
81|(Fermat).  Box lemma: for any polynomial Q over F_p of degree < M,
82|  sum_{x in {0,1}^M} (-1)^{|x|} Q(x) = 0.
83|(Proof: suffices for monomials; a monomial of degree < M omits some variable
84|x_j, and the involution flipping coordinate j pairs points with opposite signs
85|and equal values.)  Hence
86|  sum_{x : V(x) = 0, Z(x) = 0 mod p} (-1)^{|x|} = 0 in F_p.
87|The point x = 0 contributes +1, so there is a parameter point x != 0 with
88|V(x) = 0 and |S(x)| = Z(x) = 0 mod p.
89|
90|**Step 6 (conclusion).** By Step 2, S(x) is nonempty; by Step 3 its size is
91|even and at most 4p - 1; being divisible by p (odd), the size is a positive
92|even multiple of p below 4p, i.e. exactly 2p.  By Step 4 its ordered product is
93|the identity.  QED.
94|
95|## Remarks
96|
97|1. Corollary (reflection coset): any word of 3p-1 reflections in D_{2p} has an
98|   identity 2p-subword (F = 3p-1, all n_t = 0).  Finite-exact computations
99|   (m = 3..9) show 3m-1 is also the sharp threshold for composite m.
100|2. Which words are covered depends only on the R/F type pattern.  Uncovered:
101|   F - #(odd gaps) <= 2p - 2; this contains every extremal (length 4p-2)
102|   dihedral avoider found in the exhaustive D10 data (alternating odd blocks).
103|3. The 2-part (size parity) is handled ONLY through pairing inside gaps.  A
104|   general "mixed modulus" Chevalley–Warning statement that would also control
105|   parity is false (it would give D(G) = D*(G) for G = C_2^4 + C_6).
106|4. Prime powers m = p^k: the same argument with congruences mod p^k would need
107|   Brink's restricted-variable Chevalley theorem for prime-power moduli
108|   (Combinatorica 31 (2011) 127-130); its exact hypotheses have NOT been read
109|   from the source here, so the extension is CONDITIONAL and kept separate.

42|> Let G be an arbitrary group, and let G = A_1 ∪ … ∪ A_7 be an exact cover by seven disjoint nonempty left cosets of (possibly different) subgroups. Then 25 ≤ |G| ≤ 29. Each admitted order admits an example; no order outside 25..29 admits such a cover. The seven cosets cannot be required to be distinct in advance, and G is not assumed finite in the hypothesis; finiteness and 25..29 follow.

4|
5|Assume w avoids. Define e(x)=(p+1)x in Z/(2p). It is well-defined and additive because p(p+1) is divisible by2p; its values are even and congruent toxmodp, hence it is the unique even lift. E(P)=A_P+B_(P^c)=B+D_P. Therefore psi(P)=e(2u_P)−E(P).
6|
7|If u_P has an integer representative in[-floor((R−E)/2),floor(E/2)], doubling gives an even t in[-(R−E),E] with t congruent toe(2u_P)mod2p. Conversely such an even t has t/2 congruent tou_Pmodp and lies in that floor window. Equivalently psi has a representative q in[-R,0]: setq=t−E in one direction; in the other q+E is EVEN because its residue ise(2u_P), and lies in[-(R−E),E]. The complementary representatives are[1,2p−R−1].
8|
9|For increment h_i congruent toe(theta_i)−d_i mod2p, parity is h_i congruent tod_i congruent tor_i mod2. The increment interval has fewer than2pintegers and contains0, so h_i=0 iff the class vanishes; equivalently d_i is even and theta_i congruent tod_imodp. A genuine empty run has d_i0 and theta_i nonzero, so its increment is even nonzero. P0's avoider hypothesis is now explicit.
10|
11|CaseB: stretch indices of one parity are nonadjacent on the2m0-cycle since their differences are nonzeroeven while adjacent differences are±1. Flipping a proper subset of that parity changes no boundary inside a stretch, removes exactly its two incident old boundaries, and creates none. CaseC: neighbours withinJ share the initial sign and lie in the same proper stretch, so independence on those interior paths is independence on the originalcycle; old-boundary endpoints are never flipped. These justify the already-reviewed sign counts including n=2f.

46|**Theorem P: fresh full-proof check**
47|
48|The group law gives
49|\[
50|(a_1,e_1)\cdots(a_s,e_s)
51|=
52|\left(\sum_j\Bigl(\prod_{\ell<j}e_\ell\Bigr)a_j,\ \prod_j e_j\right).
53|\]
54|Thus the prefix reflection parity supplies precisely the signs used in P Step 4. The reflection contribution telescopes as printed, and a selected rotation pair contributes \(x_\pi(m_\pi-u_t)\) on the Boolean box. Consequently \(V\) has degree **at most one**, including the possibility \(V=0\).
55|
56|The size polynomial agrees with the actual selected length on Boolean points and has degree **at most two**. Therefore
57|\[
58|M=(F-1)+\sum_t\lfloor n_t/2\rfloor\ge3p-2,
59|\qquad
60|\deg Q\le3(p-1)=3p-3<M.
61|\]
62|The signed box lemma is proved within P: every monomial of degree below \(M\) omits a variable, whose flip cancels its two contributions. Fermat’s theorem makes \(Q\) the indicator of the two required congruences on the Boolean box.
63|
64|The origin contributes \(+1\), so cancellation forces another solution. Injectivity and the unique-empty-selection argument make its selection nonempty. Its length is even, divisible by the odd prime \(p\), and below \(4p\); hence it is exactly \(2p\). Its product is the identity.
65|
66|For original order, write the input word \(AB\) and its cyclic cut \(BA\). The same selected positions have products
67|\[
68|P_BP_A \quad\text{and}\quad
69|P_AP_B=P_B^{-1}(P_BP_A)P_B.
70|\]
71|Identity therefore survives restoration of the original increasing-index order, even when a constructed pair crosses that original boundary.
72|
73|Both P errata are necessary readings, and neither invalidates an HK step. No general Chevalley–Warning theorem, prime-power extension or finite computation is needed: the supplied signed-box argument is sufficient.
74|
75|P’s LF3–5 computational-checking and “not yet independently reviewed” language remains the immutable historical header. The supplied **04:22:45** acceptance records a later verification closure at its own date and scope. It does not backdate two-family verification. P’s composite-threshold remarks and conditional prime-power discussion supply no premise here.
76|
77|**General NF, GRX and every HK use of P**
78|
79|For the actual all-odd NF,
80|\[
81|F=k+2H,\qquad H=p-1-\widehat\kappa,\qquad
82|R=2p+2\widehat\kappa-k.
83|\]
84|The local NF paragraph supplies these at general \(k\); the later restricted few-odd theorem is not being extended. \(H\ge0\) gives \(\widehat\kappa\le p-1\). If \(\widehat\kappa<0\), then \(H\ge p\), and all-plus GRX permits \(2p\) reflections with zero rotations, contradicting avoidance.
85|
86|For every sign pattern,
87|\[
88|\delta=k-\Phi,\qquad C_{\max}=2H+\Phi.
89|\]
90|Substitution of \(n=2p-C\) gives exactly
91|\[
92|\max(0,2\widehat\kappa+2-\Phi)
93|\le n\le
94|\min(R,2p-\Phi),\qquad n\ \text{even}.
95|\]
96|Global sign reversal changes both the signed sum and \(T\) by a minus sign, so arbitrary patterns are legitimate. Empty gaps, empty size intervals and \(\Sigma_0=\{0\}\) are handled correctly. The interval-boundary formula \(T=y_{j'}-y_j\) matches the supplied group convention.
97|
98|Every P invocation has an actual input \(u\) of length \(4p-2\), an odd prime, and \(F\ge k\ge p+2\). Its reflection-to-reflection rotation gaps are the \(r_i\), together with internal zero gaps inside the constant reflection blocks. Hence
99|\[
100|P_{\rm gap}=\sum_i\lfloor r_i/2\rfloor,
101|\qquad
102|F+P_{\rm gap}\le3p-2,
103|\]
104|and therefore
105|\[
106|P_{\rm gap}\le p+2\widehat\kappa-k,\qquad
107|k\le p+2\widehat\kappa.
108|\]
109|
110|This validates HK LF54–59, the critical reduction LF61–65, the run structure used at LF122–128, and residual (R1) at LF182–184. The larger-\(k\) conclusion in LF138–140 and baseline attribution at LF207 have the same valid basis. **No rotation-value multiplicity is substituted for a gap length.**
111|
112|Here P’s restored “original order” is the order of its input word \(u\). Every subsequent selection is built in \(u\) and contradicts \(u\)’s avoidance. Nothing is inversely transported through normalization to \(w\).
113|
114|**The complete \(\widehat\kappa=1\) argument**
115|
116|P already excludes \(k\ge p+3\). At \(k=p+2\),
117|\[
118|H=p-2,\quad F=3p-2,\quad R=p,\quad P_{\rm gap}=0.
119|\]
120|Thus every \(r_i\) is zero or one, exactly two are empty, and there are exactly \(p\) maximal cyclic reflection runs. A zero-valued rotation remains a separating letter.
121|
122|The rotation dichotomy is exhaustive because every nonempty zero-sum of odd terms has even length, while all even lengths at least four are excluded by all-plus GRX. Any surviving nonempty zero-sum must therefore be a pair.
123|
124|Padding retains every \(h_i\). Whether a representative is selected or not, block \(i\) can supply any additional number of pairs from zero through \(h_i\). Adding these pairs preserves the ordered product. Conversely, reducing each selected block count to its parity removes at most \(H\) pairs. At \(\widehat\kappa=1\), pure representative-reflection identities of lengths four and six are consequently forbidden.
125|
126|The COLOR argument is sound. Two doubled labels must interlace; three pairwise interlacing doubled labels yield \(ABCABC\), an identity. With at most two doubles, \(p+2\) positions force exactly two doubles and all \(p\) labels present. The distinct label \(C=2A-B\) then supplies the cyclic identity \(ABAC\).
127|
128|In the zero-sum-pair branch, two zero rotations work with any signs. For a nonzero pair, three occurrences **among block labels** divide the rotation occurrences into three arcs; one misses both marked letters. Equal boundary labels give \(T=0\), and both marked rotations receive the same sign. In either case, \(n=2\) is admissible. COLOR finishes the remaining case. There is no bound on raw reflection-letter multiplicities.
129|
130|For the zero-sum-free branch, S(p) supplies positive coefficients relative to a generator. Parity makes every coefficient odd. With worker erratum’s notation,
131|\[
132|u_{\rm one}+s=p,\qquad E=W-p\le p-1,\qquad E\ge2s.
133|\]
134|For \(s\ge2\), every larger coefficient satisfies
135|\[
136|b\le p-2s+2\le p-s=u_{\rm one}.
137|\]
138|For \(s=1\), the sole exception to \(b\le u_{\rm one}\) is \(b=p\), namely \(g^{p-1}(pg)\).
139|
140|Outside that exception, every nonempty rotation bipartition has an even signed cancellation of size \(2\) through \(p-1\): either take coefficient-one occurrences from both sides, or cancel one coefficient \(b\) against \(b\) distinct coefficient-one occurrences on the other side. The signed cancellation is in the actual \(\mathbb F_p\) rotation values. No occurrence is reused.
141|
142|A repeated label in two different maximal reflection runs would then violate the equal-boundary \(\Phi=2,T=0\) test. Their palettes must be disjoint. There are \(p\) runs, and \(p+2\) blocks force one run to contain different adjacent block labels. That requires at least \(p+1\) colors, a contradiction.
143|
144|For the literal exception, the rotations are \(c^{p-1},0\), with \(c\ne0\). A four-change pattern exists without requiring all rotation runs to be nonempty. With \(A+B=p-1\), every integer \(d\in[-B,A]\) is realized using \(|d|\) appropriately signed \(c\)-letters; the zero letter corrects parity when necessary. The resulting even sizes are at most \(p-1\), and the \(p\) consecutive integers give every residue \(dc\). All these sizes are admissible at \(\Phi=4,\widehat\kappa=1\). This is a fresh GRX argument, not an ARC0 import.
145|
146|S(p) supplies the coefficient representation in the zero-sum-free argument. Once the literal exceptional rotations are given, their fresh GRX exclusion requires no additional S(p) step.
147|
148|The separate \(p=5\) statement also checks: NF has its supplied \(p\ge5\) interface; GRX and P apply; padding permits length six; four changes exist at \(k=7\); and the S(p) overlay explicitly includes \(n=10\) at secondary-restatement trust. No \(p=5\) VAC extension or computational lift is used.
149|
150|**Top diagnostic and HIGH-K-RES**
151|
152|At \(k=p+2,\widehat\kappa=p-1\), \(H=0\), all blocks are singletons and \(R=3p-4\). Since \(k\) is odd, \(0\le\Phi\le p+1\), and GRX fixes
153|\[
154|n=2p-\Phi.
155|\]
156|All these sizes are available.
157|
158|CT applies to the **all-plus** odd sequence with \(n_{\rm CT}=p-4\), because its complement has length exactly \(2p\). This gives CT-top. It supplies no automatic CT hypothesis for arbitrary signed multisets.
159|
160|For a pattern with \(\Phi\ge4\), suppose its signed maximum multiplicity were at most \(2p-3\). The proved generic pairing lemma supplies \(p-1\) disjoint unequal pairs. The required size satisfies
161|\[
162|p-1\le n\le2p-4.
163|\]
164|There are \(p-2\) unused occurrences, sufficient for the required zero through \(p-3\) fillers. Choosing one occurrence per pair and applying Cauchy–Davenport to their \(p-1\) nonzero differences reaches every residue, contradicting GRX.
165|
166|Thus every such pattern has a unique signed value of multiplicity at least \(2p-2\). Its original unsigned class contains that many rotation occurrences. Two different unsigned classes cannot both fit within \(R=3p-4\), so the class is common across the patterns. It cannot be zero: repeated block labels would give a \(\Phi=2,T=0\) pattern admitting exactly \(2p-2\) zero rotations.
167|
168|This proves the stated **common nonzero near-pair** conclusion, with at most \(p-2\) rotations outside it. At \(\Phi=2\), the same construction requires \(p-1\) fillers but has only \(p-2\). That is a failure of this argument, not a no-go theorem, full rigidity or exclusion.
169|
170|The entire residual retains all actual NF data, all \(h_i\), genuine empty gaps, zero-valued rotation letters, the full P-gap inequality, and every GRX pattern and admissible even size. Worker erratum 1 correctly limits the residual DAV sentence to the displayed redundant bound.
171|
172|The exact positive CT domain in (R3), writing its parameter as \(n_{\rm CT}\), is
173|\[
174|\max(1,R-2p)\le n_{\rm CT}\le R-2\widehat\kappa-2,
175|\qquad n_{\rm CT}\equiv R\pmod2.
176|\]
177|Equivalently, the upper endpoint is \(2p-k-2\). An empty domain supplies no CT test and does not remove that part of the residual. At the top diagnostic, it consists exactly of \(n_{\rm CT}=p-4\).
178|
179|The residual lower bound \(\widehat\kappa\ge2\) **inherits S(p)’s secondary-statement trust through HK1**. Without that import, the unclosed zero-sum-free critical branch must be retained. The standalone top arguments do not themselves use S(p).
180|

HIGH-K-RES retains its necessary-system status. HIGH-K-CORE-COVER remains unproved throughout the entire displayed band.

33|## 1. Theorem P: statement, domain, trust, order, degrees and every HK call
34|
35|**Statement and domain.** The verdict (LF5) restates proof LF10–28:
36|- p is any odd prime;
37|- the word has N≤4p−1 and F≥1;
38|- n_t are the cyclic rotation-gap lengths between consecutive reflections;
39|- if F+Σ⌊n_t/2⌋≥3p−1, the word has an identity 2p-subword in increasing original order.
40|
41|Nothing in the acceptance narrows this statement.
42|
43|**Proof re-derived by hand (LF30–93).**
44|- **Steps 1–3.** M=(F−1)+Σ⌊n_t/2⌋≥3p−2. The map from parameters to selections is injective, because u_{t+1}=u_t⊕[f_t selected] and m_π=[π selected]⊕u_t. The selection is empty only at 0. The reflection count equals the number of colour changes around the cycle, so it is even.
45|- **Step 4.** By the product formula (P erratum v2, item 2), the prefix reflection parity before gap t or before f_t is ≡u_t, so the sign is c_t=(−1)^{u_t}. I checked three identities:
46|  - (c_t−c_{t+1})/2 = u_{t+1}−u_t in all four cases.
47|  - Summation by parts works with u_1=u_{F+1}=0.
48|  - c_t[m⊕u_t] = m−u_t for u_t∈{0,1}.
49|
50|  Together these give V=Σ_{t≥2}β_t u_t+Σx_π m_π. V has degree ≤1 and may vanish identically (P erratum v1).
51|- **Step 5.** Z agrees with |S| on the box, and deg Z≤2. So deg Q≤3p−3<3p−2≤M. A monomial of degree <M omits some variable, and flipping that variable pairs terms of opposite sign. The point x=0 contributes +1, so a nonzero solution exists.
52|- **Step 6.** The selection is nonempty, has even size ≤4p−1, and its size is ≡0 mod p. So the size is exactly 2p.
53|- **Order.** Write the original word as AB and the rotated word as BA. Then P_A P_B = P_B^{−1}(P_B P_A)P_B, so the same positions give the identity in the original order (P erratum v2, item 1).
54|
55|**Effect of the overlays.** The degree wording, product formula, original-order and notation overlays are exposition only. HK uses only the contrapositive of P's statement, so none of them can affect an HK step.
56|
57|**Trust.** P is a written theorem, accepted at 2026-10-21T04:22:45 by both families and checked by a parent derivation. No computation and no unproved extension is a premise.
58|
59|The header (LF1–7: brute-force checks, "NOT yet independently reviewed") lies outside the accepted mathematical text LF8–93. So do the Remarks (LF95–109: finite data, composite sharpness, conditional Brink extension). Both are historical, not re-executed, and not used by HK.
60|
61|**Not backdated.** The HK manuscript postdates cp-04 (14:52:16), so its use of P falls within the gate's forward scope. Verdict LF13's non-backdating sentence concerns K0T/LOWER-K1, not HK.
62|
63|The first packet's path-free evidence for P (K0*/RED LF7) is superseded, not retroactively upgraded. This family's earlier citation of P's p=5 scope through K0*'s p≥5 is likewise superseded by P's own "any odd prime" domain.

110|
111|## 3. Re-derivation record (condensed)
112|
113|**General NF and GRX.** Every ℓ_i is odd, so δ=k−Φ and C_max=2H+Φ. Substituting n=2p−C into GRX LF18–19 gives (G) exactly. Because Σθ_i=0, an interval P=R_{j+1..j′} has T=y_j′−y_j. If H≥p, the all-plus pattern would give an identity, so κ̂≥0.
114|
115|**Critical cell (p≥5, k=p+2, κ̂=1).**
116|- H=p−2, F=3p−2 and R=p.
117|- There are p maximal cyclic reflection runs. A ρ_0 is a rotation letter, not an empty gap.
118|- All-plus (G) forbids zero-sums of even length in [4,p], and every nonempty zero-sum of odd terms has even length.
119|- So Y is either zero-sum-free or has a zero-sum pair. The parity of |Y| plays no role (M-1).
120|
121|**Padding.**
122|- In each block, reduce the selected count c to c mod 2; this is valid because σ_y^{c+2j}=σ_y^c.
123|- For c∈{0,1}, block i can take up to h_i extra pairs, and any total from 0 to H is realisable.
124|- So u has an identity 2p-subword exactly when the skeleton has an identity selection of even length L∈[2κ̂+2,2p].
125|- Arbitrary h_i are kept throughout; GRX sees the blocks only through 2H+Φ.
126|
127|**COLOR.**
128|- σ_aσ_bσ_cσ_d=ρ_{a−b+c−d}, so two non-interlaced doubled labels give an identity of length 4.
129|- Three pairwise-interlaced doubled labels must sit as ABCABC, whose product is (σ_Aσ_Bσ_C)²=1.
130|- The count p+2 = (#labels present) + (#doubled labels) then forces exactly two doubles and all p labels present.
131|- C=2A−B differs from A and B and occurs once. The A-to-A arc that avoids C contains a B, and the selection ABAC has product ρ_{2A−B−C}=1.
132|- Padding needs (2p−4)/2≤H and (2p−6)/2≤H, which hold for p≥5.
133|
134|The hypothesis is on **block labels**, as parent E1 states. A bound on raw reflection-letter multiplicity would contradict arbitrary h_i.
135|
136|The hypothesis also cannot simply be dropped. cp-02 LF60–62 gives the representative sequence 0,0,0,1,…,p−1. That note is not a premise, but I rechecked it by hand: the sequence has no pure-reflection identity of even length ≥4, because alternating sums of a weakly increasing sequence lie in (−p,0]. This is why §3 has to handle a label occurring three or more times through rotations.
137|
138|**§3 (Y has a zero-sum pair).**
139|- **x=0:** since k>p, some block label repeats. The Φ=2, T=0 interval pattern together with the two zero letters gives n=2∈[2,p].
140|- **x≠0, some label occurs ≥3 times:** three occurrences cut the cycle into three arcs, and one arc misses both marked letters. Taking P to be that arc, both letters get sign +1 and cancel.
141|- **Otherwise:** every label occurs at most twice, and COLOR plus padding applies.
142|- No S(p) is used in §3.
143|
144|**§4, coefficients.** Parity is a homomorphism Z_{2p}→Z_2, so every a_i is odd (M-3). Put E=W−p≤p−1.
145|- s≥2: b≤E−2(s−1)+1≤p−2s+2≤p−s=u_one.
146|- s=1: b≤p. The case b=p is exactly (X); otherwise b≤p−2.
147|- s=0: every coefficient is 1.
148|
149|The signed cancellations are either one coefficient-1 term on each side (size 2), or b against b coefficient-1 terms (size b+1, which is even and ≤p−1). Distinct occurrences are used throughout.
150|
151|**§4, palettes.** Suppose equal labels sit in different maximal runs. Then both arcs between them contain rotations, T=0, and the cancellation size lies in [2,p−1]⊂[2,p]. So the p palettes are disjoint. There are p+2 blocks in p runs, and the empty-run rule forces some palette to have size ≥2. That would need at least p+1 colours, a contradiction.
152|
153|**(X).**
154|- g=(1,c) with c≠0, and pg=(1,0), so the rotations are ρ_c^{p−1}ρ_0.
155|- Take two nonadjacent runs as the minus set; this gives Φ=4 and exists because k≥7.
156|- For each d∈[−B,A], select |d| signed c-letters, adding ρ_0 when |d| is odd. The size is even and ≤p−1, and the values dc cover F_p.
157|- At Φ=4, (G) allows n∈[0,min(p,2p−4)]=[0,p] for p≥5.
158|
159|S(p) is used to *reach* (X). The deduction from (X)'s literal rotations is pure GRX.
160|
161|**p=5.**
162|- NF (stated for p≥5), GRX and P (any odd prime) all apply.
163|- S(5) is the n=10 case of S(p) erratum M-1/M-2.
164|- Padding needs 3≤H=3 and 2≤3.
165|- Bipartition sizes are ≤4.
166|- The k=7 cycle has nonadjacent runs.
167|
168|**Top family.** Here H=0, F=k, R=3p−4, Φ≤p+1 and n=2p−Φ.
169|- CT applies with κ=p−1, n_CT=p−4≥1 and |Y|−n_CT=2p, giving a capped sum ≤2p−6. This is the only (R3) test in this family.
170|- Lemma M (re-proved inline in HK) with q=p−1 needs μ≤2p−3. For Φ≥4, n∈[p−1,2p−4], so the fillers needed, n−p+1, lie in [0,p−3], within the p−2 available. Cauchy–Davenport on p−1 nonzero differences covers F_p. Hence μ≥2p−2.
171|- The heavy value is unique, since 4p−4>R. Its unsigned class is common to all Φ≥4 patterns, and such patterns exist.
172|- The class is nonzero. Otherwise 2p−2 zero letters plus a repeated label would give a Φ=2 pattern with n=2p−2 and T=0.
173|- So at most p−2 letters lie outside the class.
174|- At Φ=2 the method needs p−1 fillers but only p−2 remain. HK correctly treats this as a failed step, not a no-go.
175|
176|**HIGH-K-RES.**
177|- κ̂≤p−1 follows from H≥0.
178|- κ̂≥1 follows from P.
179|- κ̂≠1 follows from HK1, and therefore depends on S(p).
180|- k≤p+2κ̂, and R≥p follows.
181|- (R1)–(R3) are literal. The (R3) domain is max(1,2κ̂−k)≤q≤2p−k−2 with q≡k mod 2. When it is empty, that k is not removed.
182|- Every surviving NF satisfies (R0)–(R3), so the residual is **necessary**. Nothing beyond that is credited.
183|
